Let's answer.

Yes — those files are leftover scaffolding placeholders from the initial repo setup. They are not part of the implemented backend and are safe to leave empty or delete. The current working API does not reference them, and none are registered in DI, so they don’t affect the build or runtime.

Quick breakdown
File	Status / Why it’s empty	Recommendation
RoomController.cs	Rooms are handled inside PropertyController (/api/properties/{id}/rooms). No separate RoomController was implemented.	Leave empty or delete.
UploadPaymentRequest.cs	Payment proof uses UploadProofOfPaymentRequest.cs. This was an older placeholder name.	Delete.
TenantDashboardFacade.cs	Façade pattern was scaffolded but never implemented. The tenant dashboard will aggregate data via API calls directly.	Leave empty or delete.
ISmartAllocationService.cs	Smart allocation was planned but not implemented.	Leave empty or delete.
SmartAllocationService.cs	Same as above — unused placeholder.	Leave empty or delete.
IRoomService.cs	Room logic lives in PropertyService. No separate room service was implemented.	Leave empty or delete.
RoomService.cs	Same as above — unused placeholder.	Leave empty or delete.
StringExtensions.cs	Shared helper placeholder; no extension methods were needed.	Leave empty or delete.
DashboardResponse.cs	Report dashboard uses DashboardReportResponse.cs. This is an older placeholder.	Delete.
Are empty .cs files a problem?
No. The C# compiler accepts empty source files. If they contain only a placeholder class or interface with a // TODO, they still compile fine. They simply sit in the assembly unused.

Should you delete them?
For a clean, production-ready repo, yes — delete the unused ones. It avoids confusion for teammates and reviewers. Before deleting, you can run this to confirm nothing references them:

powershell
Get-ChildItem -Recurse -Filter *.cs |
  Select-String -Pattern "RoomController|UploadPaymentRequest|TenantDashboardFacade|ISmartAllocationService|IRoomService|SmartAllocationService|RoomService|StringExtensions|DashboardResponse" |
  Where-Object { $_.Path -notmatch '\\(bin|obj)\\' }
If the only matches are the files themselves, they are dead code and safe to remove.

What to keep
Keep Room entity, RoomResponse, CreateRoomRequest, and the room endpoints inside PropertyController.

Keep DashboardReportResponse.cs — that’s the one actually used by /api/reports/dashboard.

So: yes, they’re supposed to be empty — or better, deleted. They’re not required for the backend to work.

Part 8: Common Failures & Fixes
Symptom	Cause	Fix
Frontend shows blank page	API not running or wrong URL	Check src/UniCribz.Web/appsettings.json → Api:BaseUrl = http://localhost:5125
CORS error in browser console	Origin not allowed	Add Web's origin to App:AllowedOrigins in appsettings.json and restart API
401 on every authenticated request	JWT not being sent	Check DevTools → Network → request headers. If missing, login logic in Sam's controller isn't storing the token
429 Too Many Requests	Rate limiter hit (100/min/IP)	Wait 60 seconds. Or throttle frontend API calls
Stripe webhook not received	Webhook secret mismatch	Verify Stripe:WebhookSecret matches Stripe dashboard
Email not sent	SendGrid API key invalid	Test at SendGrid dashboard first, then re-set dotnet user-secrets
GET /api/maps/geocode → 500	IGoogleMapsService not registered	Uncomment the DI line in ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs
Postgres connection refused	Docker not running	Start Docker Desktop, docker-compose up -d, wait for healthy
/health/ready → Unhealthy	Postgres or Redis down	Restart containers
Swagger "Unable to render"	Cached OpenAPI spec	Hard refresh (Ctrl+Shift+R), or clear browser cache
Part 9: Database Reset (If Tests Pollute State)
If test runs leave the DB in a messy state, wipe and re-seed:

powershell
# Stop the API first (Ctrl+C in Terminal 2)

docker exec -it unicribz-postgres psql -U postgres -d UniCribzDb -c "DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE; CREATE SCHEMA public;"

cd src\UniCribz.Api
dotnet ef database update --project ../UniCribz.Data --startup-project .
dotnet run
On startup the seeder will re-insert the 3 users + sample property.

using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using UniCribz.Web.Services;
using UniCribz.Web.ViewModels;

namespace UniCribz.Web.Controllers;

public class PropertyController : Controller
{
    private readonly IApiClient _api;

    public PropertyController(IApiClient api)
    {
        _api = api;
    }

    public async Task<IActionResult> Search(string? city, decimal? maxPrice, string? sort)
    {
        var properties = await _api.SearchPropertiesAsync(city, maxPrice) ?? new();

        // each card costs one extra API call (for the price), so cap the page
        var cards = await PropertyCards.BuildAsync(_api, properties.OrderBy(p => p.Name).Take(12));

        cards = sort switch
        {
            "available" => cards.OrderByDescending(c => c.Property.AvailableRooms).ToList(),
            "price" => cards.OrderBy(c => c.MinRent ?? decimal.MaxValue).ToList(),
            _ => cards
        };

        return View(new PropertySearchViewModel
        {
            City = city,
            MaxPrice = maxPrice,
            Sort = sort ?? "name",
            Results = cards
        });
    }

    public async Task<IActionResult> Details(Guid id)
    {
        var property = await _api.GetPropertyAsync(id);
        if (property is null)
            return NotFound();

        var rooms = await _api.GetRoomsAsync(id) ?? new();

        return View(new PropertyDetailsViewModel { Property = property, Rooms = rooms });
    }
}
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Filters;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.ModelBinding;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.ViewFeatures;
using UniCribz.Web.Services;

namespace UniCribz.Web.Filters;

/// <summary>
/// Catches ApiException thrown by page loads. An expired/invalid token sends the user to the
/// login page; anything else shows a friendly error page. (Form posts handle their own
/// ApiExceptions so the form can be redisplayed with the message.)
/// </summary>
public class ApiExceptionFilter : IAsyncExceptionFilter
{
    public async Task OnExceptionAsync(ExceptionContext context)
    {
        if (context.Exception is not ApiException ex)
            return;

        context.ExceptionHandled = true;

        if (ex.StatusCode == 401)
        {
            await context.HttpContext.SignOutAsync();
            var request = context.HttpContext.Request;
            context.Result = new RedirectToActionResult("Login", "Account", new
            {
                returnUrl = request.Method == "GET" ? request.Path + request.QueryString : null,
                expired = true
            });
            return;
        }

        var viewData = new ViewDataDictionary<ApiException>(new EmptyModelMetadataProvider(), context.ModelState)
        {
            Model = ex
        };

        context.Result = new ViewResult
        {
            ViewName = "ApiError",
            ViewData = viewData,
            StatusCode = ex.StatusCode == 404 ? 404 : 502
        };
    }
}
namespace UniCribz.Web.Services;

/// <summary>Raised when the UniCribz API returns an error (or cannot be reached).</summary>
public class ApiException : Exception
{
    public int StatusCode { get; }

    public ApiException(int statusCode, string message) : base(message)
    {
        StatusCode = statusCode;
    }
}
using UniCribz.Web.Models.Api;
using UniCribz.Web.ViewModels;

namespace UniCribz.Web.Services;

/// <summary>
/// The API's property response has no price, so each card looks up its rooms and shows
/// the lowest rent (preferring rooms that are currently available).
/// </summary>
public static class PropertyCards
{
    public static async Task<List<PropertyCardViewModel>> BuildAsync(IApiClient api, IEnumerable<PropertyDto> properties)
    {
        var tasks = properties.Select(async property =>
        {
            decimal? minRent = null;
            try
            {
                var rooms = await api.GetRoomsAsync(property.Id) ?? new List<RoomDto>();
                var pool = rooms.Where(r => r.Status == "Available").ToList();
                if (pool.Count == 0) pool = rooms;
                if (pool.Count > 0) minRent = pool.Min(r => r.MonthlyRent);
            }
            catch (ApiException)
            {
                // a failed price lookup shouldn't hide the property
            }

            return new PropertyCardViewModel { Property = property, MinRent = minRent };
        });

        return (await Task.WhenAll(tasks)).ToList();
    }
}
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using UniCribz.Web.Services;
using UniCribz.Web.ViewModels;

namespace UniCribz.Web.ViewComponents;

/// <summary>
/// The "Featured properties" strip on the home page. It loads its own data, so the existing
/// HomeController doesn't need to change. If the API is down the home page still loads.
/// </summary>
public class FeaturedPropertiesViewComponent : ViewComponent
{
    private readonly IApiClient _api;

    public FeaturedPropertiesViewComponent(IApiClient api)
    {
        _api = api;
    }

    public async Task<IViewComponentResult> InvokeAsync(int count = 3)
    {
        try
        {
            var properties = await _api.SearchPropertiesAsync(null, null) ?? new();
            var cards = await PropertyCards.BuildAsync(_api, properties.OrderByDescending(p => p.AvailableRooms).Take(count));
            return View(new FeaturedPropertiesViewModel { Cards = cards });
        }
        catch (ApiException)
        {
            return View(new FeaturedPropertiesViewModel { Unavailable = true });
        }
    }
}

public class FeaturedPropertiesViewModel
{
    public List<PropertyCardViewModel> Cards { get; set; } = new();
    public bool Unavailable { get; set; }
}
using System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations;

namespace UniCribz.Web.ViewModels;

public class LoginViewModel
{
    [Required(ErrorMessage = "Email is required.")]
    [EmailAddress(ErrorMessage = "Enter a valid email address.")]
    public string Email { get; set; } = "";

    [Required(ErrorMessage = "Password is required.")]
    [DataType(DataType.Password)]
    public string Password { get; set; } = "";

    public string? ReturnUrl { get; set; }
}

public class RegisterViewModel
{
    [Required(ErrorMessage = "Full name is required.")]
    [StringLength(150, MinimumLength = 2, ErrorMessage = "Enter your full name.")]
    [Display(Name = "Full name")]
    public string FullName { get; set; } = "";

    [Required(ErrorMessage = "Email is required.")]
    [EmailAddress(ErrorMessage = "Enter a valid email address.")]
    [StringLength(255)]
    public string Email { get; set; } = "";

    [StringLength(20)]
    [Display(Name = "Phone number")]
    public string? PhoneNumber { get; set; }

    [StringLength(50)]
    [Display(Name = "Student number")]
    public string? StudentNumber { get; set; }

    [Required(ErrorMessage = "Password is required.")]
    [RegularExpression(@"^(?=.*[a-z])(?=.*[A-Z])(?=.*\d).{8,}$",
        ErrorMessage = "Use at least 8 characters with an uppercase letter, a lowercase letter and a digit.")]
    [DataType(DataType.Password)]
    public string Password { get; set; } = "";

    [Required(ErrorMessage = "Please confirm your password.")]
    [Compare(nameof(Password), ErrorMessage = "Passwords do not match.")]
    [DataType(DataType.Password)]
    [Display(Name = "Confirm password")]
    public string ConfirmPassword { get; set; } = "";
}

🚨 Critical Fix Before You Test
src/UniCribz.Web/appsettings.json has the wrong API URL
Your file has:

json
"Api": { "BaseUrl": "https://localhost:7285" }
But the API in Dev runs on http://localhost:5125 (we saw this in all the API logs). With the current value, every frontend API call will fail with connection refused and all pages will show the ApiError view.

Fix — update src/UniCribz.Web/appsettings.json:

json
{
  "Api": {
    "BaseUrl": "http://localhost:5125"
  },
  "Logging": {
    "LogLevel": {
      "Default": "Information",
      "Microsoft.AspNetCore": "Warning"
    }
  },
  "AllowedHosts": "*",
  "ConnectionStrings": {
    "DefaultConnection": "Host=localhost;Port=5432;Database=UniCribzDb;Username=postgres;Password=UniCribzDev123!"
  }
}
Just change the one line. Save. This is the #1 cause of "the frontend loads but nothing works".

⚠️ Things to Test (may need small fixes)
1. Antiforgery on POSTs
Your AddUniCribzWeb registers AutoValidateAntiforgeryTokenAttribute globally. Every POST from a form needs a valid antiforgery token. Your views use <form asp-action="..." method="post"> which auto-emits the token. This should be fine.

But if any POST returns 400 with an empty body during testing, add one line to Program.cs after UseRouting():

csharp
app.UseAntiforgery();
2. Slot time parsing (ViewingController)
SlotFormat = "yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss'Z'" matches what the API returns. If you see "That time slot isn't valid", check the raw JSON from GET /api/viewings/available in Swagger — the startTime field should look like 2026-11-15T09:00:00Z. If it's missing the Z, tell me and we'll adjust.

3. Tenant login redirect
The seeder account tenant@unicribz.co.za has no lease attached. After login as Tenant, /Tenant/Dashboard will render "You don't have a lease yet" — correct behaviour, but you'll need to create a lease as admin before the tenant dashboard shows real data.

4. AutoValidateAntiforgeryTokenAttribute on /Account/Login
Login itself is a POST form → needs a token. The <form asp-action="Login" method="post"> in Views/Account/Login.cshtml provides it. Fine.

5. Session cookie and logout
Logout calls HttpContext.Session.Clear() and SignOutAsync. That clears the visitor's session-based application/viewing lists. Expected.

Accept: {{json}}

### 9.4 Admin resolves → Resolved (2)

Authorization: Bearer {{adminToken}}
Content-Type: {{json}}

{
  "status": 2,
  "resolutionNotes": "Spoke to the neighbour, warning issued."
}

Before you run this suite

Start the API: cd src\UniCribz.Api && dotnet run

In Visual Studio, open the .http file, click Run All

Reset the DB for a clean run
powershell

cd src\UniCribz.Api
dotnet ef database update --project ../UniCribz.Data --startup-project .

dotnet run

Let me scan through for problems:

CRITICAL BUG #1: Forbid(string) misuse causing 400 errors

Looking at these errors:

text
[14:47:48 ERR] Unhandled exception for GET /api/payments/54a45ed8-323b-4aaf-9173-07239cd43589/receipt
System.InvalidOperationException: No authentication handler is registered for the scheme 'You can only view your own receipts.'. The registered schemes are: Bearer.
and

text
[14:47:51 ERR] Unhandled exception for POST /api/leases/0644e964-b6a5-4486-b3cb-873dff3a1aca/sign
System.InvalidOperationException: No authentication handler is registered for the scheme 'You can only sign your own lease.'.
This is the classic Forbid(string) mistake. When you call Forbid("some message"), ASP.NET interprets the string as an authentication scheme name, not a message. This throws an InvalidOperationException at runtime and returns 400 instead of 403.

The code should be return Forbid(); or return StatusCode(403, new { error = ex.Message });.

Files affected:

PaymentController.cs — Forbid(ex.Message) on receipt

LeaseController.cs — Forbid(ex.Message) on sign

Possibly others

CRITICAL BUG #2: Weak password validation not enforced

text
[14:48:22 INF] New user registered: weakpass@example.com
...
[14:48:23 INF] POST /api/auth/register → 201
The password was abc123 which should fail validation (needs uppercase, lowercase, digit). But it was accepted. The FluentValidation validator isn't running.

Issue 1: FluentValidation not wired up
Looking at Program.cs from earlier:

csharp
builder.Services.AddApplicationServices();
And ServiceCollectionExtensions registers validators:

csharp
services.AddScoped<IValidator<RegisterRequest>, RegisterRequestValidator>();
services.AddScoped<IValidator<LoginRequest>, LoginRequestValidator>();
services.AddScoped<IValidator<CreateApplicationRequest>, CreateApplicationRequestValidator>();
But those registrations alone don't make ASP.NET Core actually run the validators. You need either:

FluentValidation.AspNetCore model binding filter (older approach)

Or manually inject IValidator<T> into controllers and call Validate

Or use SharpGrip.FluentValidation.AutoValidation

Since FluentValidation.AspNetCore is deprecated in recent versions, and the newer approach is to use SharpGrip.FluentValidation.AutoValidation.Mvc, but actually the user has FluentValidation.AspNetCore --version 11.* which includes auto-validation via AddFluentValidationAutoValidation().

Actually the cleanest fix is to add:

csharp
builder.Services.AddFluentValidationAutoValidation();
But this needs using FluentValidation.AspNetCore; and depending on the version, the API changed.

Actually — for FluentValidation 11.x, the correct approach is:

csharp
services.AddFluentValidationAutoValidation();
services.AddValidatorsFromAssemblyContaining<RegisterRequestValidator>();
Or use SharpGrip.FluentValidation.AutoValidation.

🐛 Bug #1: FluentValidation Is Not Running
Symptoms in your log:

Line	What happened	What should happen
New user registered: weakpass@example.com → 201	Password abc123 (no uppercase) accepted	400 with "Password must contain an uppercase letter"
New user registered: not-an-email → 201	Invalid email accepted	400 with "A valid email address is required"
Property created: (a22e97ae-...) → 201	Empty property name accepted	400 with "Name is required"
POST /api/applications → 201 (past date test)	Past PreferredMoveInDate accepted	400 with "Move-in date must be in the future"
Your validators exist (RegisterRequestValidator, LoginRequestValidator, CreateApplicationRequestValidator), and they're registered in ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs — but nothing tells ASP.NET to actually run them before the action executes.

Fix
Open src/UniCribz.Api/Program.cs and add these two lines right after builder.Services.AddApplicationServices();:

csharp
// FluentValidation — runs validators automatically before controller actions
builder.Services.AddFluentValidationAutoValidation();
builder.Services.AddFluentValidationClientsideAdapters();
Add the using at the top (alongside the existing usings):

csharp
using FluentValidation.AspNetCore;
Then update ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs to register all validators from the assembly (cleaner than one-by-one):

csharp
using FluentValidation;
// ... other usings ...

public static IServiceCollection AddApplicationServices(this IServiceCollection services)
{
    // ... your existing registrations ...

    // Replace the individual AddScoped<IValidator<...>> lines with this:
    services.AddValidatorsFromAssemblyContaining<RegisterRequestValidator>();

    return services;
}

🐛 Bug #2: Forbid(string) Causes 400 Instead of 403
Symptoms in your log:

text
[14:47:48 ERR] System.InvalidOperationException: No authentication handler is registered 
for the scheme 'You can only view your own receipts.'. The registered schemes are: Bearer.
text
[14:47:51 ERR] System.InvalidOperationException: No authentication handler is registered 
for the scheme 'You can only sign your own lease.'.
Two problems:

Forbid("message") — the string argument is interpreted as an authentication scheme name, not a message. That's why you see "scheme 'You can only view your own receipts.'".

It throws → middleware catches it → returns 400 instead of the intended 403.

Fix
Open src/UniCribz.Api/Controllers/PaymentController.cs — find the Receipt action:

csharp
catch (UnauthorizedAccessException ex) { return Forbid(ex.Message); }  // ❌
Replace with:

csharp
catch (UnauthorizedAccessException ex) 
{ 
    return StatusCode(StatusCodes.Status403Forbidden, new { error = ex.Message }); 
}
Do the same in every other controller where you have return Forbid(ex.Message);. Search for it:

powershell
Get-ChildItem src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\*.cs | Select-String "Forbid\("
Fix each occurrence. Files you'll likely touch:

PaymentController.cs — Receipt action

LeaseController.cs — Sign action

Plain return Forbid(); (no argument) is fine — it returns 403 with no message. Only Forbid(string) is broken.

🐛 Bug #3: GoogleMaps Endpoints Return 404
Symptom in your log:

text
[14:48:17 INF] GET /api/maps/geocode → 404

Diagnose
powershell
Test-Path src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\GoogleMapsController.cs
If it returns False — the file doesn't exist. Create it:

csharp
// src/UniCribz.Api/Controllers/GoogleMapsController.cs
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authorization;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using UniCribz.Api.Services.Interfaces;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Controllers;

[ApiController]
[Route("api/maps")]
[AllowAnonymous]
public class GoogleMapsController : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly IGoogleMapsService _service;

    public GoogleMapsController(IGoogleMapsService service)
    {
        _service = service;
    }

    /// <summary>Geocode an address to lat/lng.</summary>
    [HttpGet("geocode")]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Geocode([FromQuery] string address)
    {
        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(address))
            return BadRequest(new { error = "Address is required." });

        var result = await _service.GeocodeAsync(address);
        return result is null ? NotFound() : Ok(result);
    }

    /// <summary>Nearby places (universities, shops, etc.).</summary>
    [HttpGet("nearby")]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Nearby(
        [FromQuery] double lat,
        [FromQuery] double lng,
        [FromQuery] string type = "university",
        [FromQuery] int radius = 3000)
    {
        var results = await _service.GetNearbyAsync(lat, lng, type, radius);
        return Ok(results);
    }
}
If it returns True — the file exists but the service isn't registered. Open src/UniCribz.Api/Extensions/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs and check for:

The Fix — Same Change in Every File
Replace every occurrence of:

csharp
catch (UnauthorizedAccessException ex) { return Forbid(ex.Message); }
with:

csharp
catch (UnauthorizedAccessException ex)
{
    return StatusCode(StatusCodes.Status403Forbidden, new { error = ex.Message });

Fastest Way — Run This Script
Run from the repo root. It rewrites each file, doing the exact replacement:

powershell
$files = @(
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\ApplicationController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\LeaseController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\NotificationController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\PaymentController.cs"
)

$oldPattern = 'catch (UnauthorizedAccessException ex) { return Forbid(ex.Message); }'
$newCode = @'
catch (UnauthorizedAccessException ex)
        {
            return StatusCode(StatusCodes.Status403Forbidden, new { error = ex.Message });
        }
'@

foreach ($file in $files) {
    $content = Get-Content $file -Raw
    if ($content.Contains($oldPattern)) {
        $content = $content.Replace($oldPattern, $newCode)
        Set-Content -Path $file -Value $content -Encoding UTF8
        Write-Host "  Fixed: $file" -ForegroundColor Green
    } else {
        Write-Host "  No exact match in: $file (fix manually)" -ForegroundColor Yellow
    }
}

🐛 Bug #1 — CurrentUserId Crashes When Claims Are Missing
Log evidence:

text
[15:17:27 ERR] System.ArgumentNullException: Value cannot be null. (Parameter 'input')
   at System.Guid.Parse(String input)
   at UniCribz.Api.Controllers.LeaseController.get_CurrentUserId() line 18
Guid.Parse(null) throws. This affects every controller that reads user ID. Even though [Authorize] blocks most unauth requests, edge cases (expired claims, malformed tokens) will crash.

The Fix — Create a Shared Helper
Create src/UniCribz.Api/Extensions/ClaimsPrincipalExtensions.cs:

csharp
using System.Security.Claims;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Extensions;

public static class ClaimsPrincipalExtensions
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Safely reads the user id from the JWT claims. Returns Guid.Empty when the claim
    /// is missing or malformed — never throws.
    /// </summary>
    public static Guid GetUserId(this ClaimsPrincipal principal)
    {
        var claim = principal.FindFirstValue(ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier)
                    ?? principal.FindFirstValue("sub");

        return Guid.TryParse(claim, out var id) ? id : Guid.Empty;
    }
}
Then Update Every Controller
Replace this pattern in all 9 controllers:

csharp
private Guid CurrentUserId =>
    Guid.Parse(User.FindFirstValue(ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier) ?? User.FindFirstValue("sub")!);
With:

csharp
private Guid CurrentUserId => User.GetUserId();
Fastest — Run This Script
Save as fix-userid.ps1 and run from repo root:

powershell
$controllers = @(
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\LeaseController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\PaymentController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\ApplicationController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\MaintenanceController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\ComplaintController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\NotificationController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\ViewingController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\PropertyController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\AnnouncementController.cs"
)

$oldPattern = 'Guid.Parse(User.FindFirstValue(ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier) ?? User.FindFirstValue("sub")!)'
$newPattern = 'User.GetUserId()'

foreach ($file in $controllers) {
    if (-not (Test-Path $file)) { Write-Host "  skip: $file" -ForegroundColor DarkGray; continue }
    $content = Get-Content $file -Raw
    if ($content.Contains($oldPattern)) {
        $content = $content.Replace($oldPattern, $newPattern)
        # Add the using if not present
        if ($content -notmatch 'using UniCribz\.Api\.Extensions;') {
            $content = $content -replace '(using UniCribz\.Api\.Services\.Interfaces;)', "`$1`nusing UniCribz.Api.Extensions;"
        }
        Set-Content -Path $file -Value $content -Encoding UTF8
        Write-Host "  Fixed: $file" -ForegroundColor Green
    } else {
        Write-Host "  No match: $file" -ForegroundColor Yellow
    }
}

Write-Host "`nDone." -ForegroundColor Cyan

🐛 Bug #2 — Property Creation Allows Empty Name

The Fix — Add the Missing Validators
Create src/UniCribz.Api/Validators/CreatePropertyRequestValidator.cs:

csharp
using FluentValidation;
using UniCribz.Api.DTOs.Requests;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Validators;

public class CreatePropertyRequestValidator : AbstractValidator<CreatePropertyRequest>
{
    public CreatePropertyRequestValidator()
    {
        RuleFor(x => x.Name)
            .NotEmpty().WithMessage("Name is required.")
            .MaximumLength(200);

        RuleFor(x => x.Description)
            .NotEmpty().WithMessage("Description is required.")
            .MaximumLength(4000);

        RuleFor(x => x.AddressLine1).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Street address is required.");
        RuleFor(x => x.Suburb).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Suburb is required.");
        RuleFor(x => x.City).NotEmpty().WithMessage("City is required.");
        RuleFor(x => x.Province).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Province is required.");
        RuleFor(x => x.PropertyType).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Property type is required.");
    }
}
Create src/UniCribz.Api/Validators/CreateLeaseRequestValidator.cs:

csharp
using FluentValidation;
using UniCribz.Api.DTOs.Requests;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Validators;

public class CreateLeaseRequestValidator : AbstractValidator<CreateLeaseRequest>
{
    public CreateLeaseRequestValidator()
    {
        RuleFor(x => x.TenantId).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.RoomId).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.MonthlyRent).GreaterThan(0).WithMessage("Monthly rent must be positive.");
        RuleFor(x => x.DepositAmount).GreaterThanOrEqualTo(0);
        RuleFor(x => x.EndDate)
            .GreaterThan(x => x.StartDate)
            .WithMessage("End date must be after start date.");
    }
}
Create src/UniCribz.Api/Validators/CreateMaintenanceRequestValidator.cs:

csharp

        RuleFor(x => x.Title).NotEmpty().MaximumLength(150);
        RuleFor(x => x.Description).NotEmpty().MaximumLength(2000);
        RuleFor(x => x.Category).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.Priority).NotEmpty();

csharp
var result = await _service.GeocodeAsync(address);

csharp
using System.Security.Claims;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Extensions;

public static class ClaimsPrincipalExtensions
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Safely reads the user id from the JWT claims. Returns Guid.Empty when the claim
    /// is missing or malformed — never throws.
    /// </summary>
    public static Guid GetUserId(this ClaimsPrincipal principal)
    {
        var claim = principal.FindFirstValue(ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier)
                    ?? principal.FindFirstValue("sub");

        return Guid.TryParse(claim, out var id) ? id : Guid.Empty;
    }
}
Save (Ctrl+S).

Step 2 — Add the using to Every Controller
The extension method lives in namespace UniCribz.Api.Extensions. Every controller that calls User.GetUserId() needs to import it.

Run this script — it adds the using to every controller that doesn't have it yet:

powershell
$controllers = @(
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\LeaseController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\PaymentController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\ApplicationController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\MaintenanceController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\ComplaintController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\NotificationController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\ViewingController.cs",
    "src\UniCribz.Api\Controllers\AnnouncementController.cs"
)

foreach ($file in $controllers) {
    if (-not (Test-Path $file)) { continue }
    $content = Get-Content $file -Raw

    if ($content -notmatch 'using UniCribz\.Api\.Extensions;') {
        # Insert the using after the LAST existing using statement
        $content = $content -replace '(using [^\n]+;\r?\n)(?!.*using )', "`$1using UniCribz.Api.Extensions;`r`n"
        Set-Content -Path $file -Value $content -Encoding UTF8
        Write-Host "  Added using: $file" -ForegroundColor Green
    } else {
        Write-Host "  Already has using: $file" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
    }
}
Write-Host "`nDone." -ForegroundColor Cyan
Expected output: 8 lines of "Added using: ..." or "Already has using: ...".

Fix — Create the 5 Missing Validators
Run this one script from the repo root. It writes each validator file:

powershell
$validators = @{
    "CreatePropertyRequestValidator" = @'
using FluentValidation;
using UniCribz.Api.DTOs.Requests;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Validators;

public class CreatePropertyRequestValidator : AbstractValidator<CreatePropertyRequest>
{
    public CreatePropertyRequestValidator()
    {
        RuleFor(x => x.Name).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Name is required.").MaximumLength(200);
        RuleFor(x => x.Description).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Description is required.").MaximumLength(4000);
        RuleFor(x => x.AddressLine1).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Street address is required.");
        RuleFor(x => x.Suburb).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Suburb is required.");
        RuleFor(x => x.City).NotEmpty().WithMessage("City is required.");
        RuleFor(x => x.Province).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Province is required.");
        RuleFor(x => x.PropertyType).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Property type is required.");
    }
}
'@

    "CreateLeaseRequestValidator" = @'
using FluentValidation;
using UniCribz.Api.DTOs.Requests;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Validators;

public class CreateLeaseRequestValidator : AbstractValidator<CreateLeaseRequest>
{
    public CreateLeaseRequestValidator()
    {
        RuleFor(x => x.TenantId).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.RoomId).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.MonthlyRent).GreaterThan(0).WithMessage("Monthly rent must be positive.");
        RuleFor(x => x.DepositAmount).GreaterThanOrEqualTo(0);
        RuleFor(x => x.EndDate).GreaterThan(x => x.StartDate)
            .WithMessage("End date must be after start date.");
    }
}
'@

    "CreateMaintenanceRequestValidator" = @'
using FluentValidation;
using UniCribz.Api.DTOs.Requests;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Validators;

public class CreateMaintenanceRequestValidator : AbstractValidator<CreateMaintenanceRequest>
{
    public CreateMaintenanceRequestValidator()
    {
        RuleFor(x => x.RoomId).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.Title).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Title is required.").MaximumLength(150);
        RuleFor(x => x.Description).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Description is required.").MaximumLength(2000);
        RuleFor(x => x.Category).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.Priority).NotEmpty();
    }
}
'@

    "CreateComplaintRequestValidator" = @'
using FluentValidation;
using UniCribz.Api.DTOs.Requests;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Validators;

public class CreateComplaintRequestValidator : AbstractValidator<CreateComplaintRequest>
{
    public CreateComplaintRequestValidator()
    {
        RuleFor(x => x.Subject).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Subject is required.").MaximumLength(200);
        RuleFor(x => x.Description).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Description is required.").MaximumLength(2000);
        RuleFor(x => x.Category).NotEmpty();
    }
}
'@

    "CreateAnnouncementRequestValidator" = @'
using FluentValidation;
using UniCribz.Api.DTOs.Requests;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Validators;

public class CreateAnnouncementRequestValidator : AbstractValidator<CreateAnnouncementRequest>
{
    public CreateAnnouncementRequestValidator()
    {
        RuleFor(x => x.Title).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Title is required.").MaximumLength(200);
        RuleFor(x => x.Content).NotEmpty().WithMessage("Content is required.").MaximumLength(4000);
        RuleFor(x => x.Category).NotEmpty();
    }
}
'@

    "InitiatePaymentRequestValidator" = @'
using FluentValidation;
using UniCribz.Api.DTOs.Requests;

namespace UniCribz.Api.Validators;

public class InitiatePaymentRequestValidator : AbstractValidator<InitiatePaymentRequest>
{
    public InitiatePaymentRequestValidator()
    {
        RuleFor(x => x.LeaseId).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.Amount).GreaterThan(0).WithMessage("Amount must be positive.");
        RuleFor(x => x.PaymentType).NotEmpty();
        RuleFor(x => x.Method).NotEmpty();
    }
}
'@
}

foreach ($name in $validators.Keys) {
    $path = "src\UniCribz.Api\Validators\$name.cs"
    Set-Content -Path $path -Value $validators[$name] -Encoding UTF8
    Write-Host "  Created: $path" -ForegroundColor Green
}

Write-Host "`nDone." -ForegroundColor Cyan

Why
The pipeline registration was already correct:

csharp
services.AddValidatorsFromAssemblyContaining<RegisterRequestValidator>();
This scans the assembly containing RegisterRequestValidator and registers every AbstractValidator<T> it finds. Since only 3 validators existed on disk, only 3 were registered. Adding the 5 files fixes it — no change to ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs is needed.
